## Claude  `16:20:16`

Project is complete at `F:\turret2`. Both build chains compile and all five parts export cleanly.

## What was built

**Firmware** (`firmware/`, PlatformIO, espressif32 6.13.0 = Arduino core 2.0.17)
- Camera at 240x240 RGB565; one detector task feeds face detection (esp-dl MSR01+MNP01, largest face) or motion detection (30x30 cell luma diff, centroid), draws the overlay, JPEG-encodes, publishes to a shared frame store.
- 50 Hz control task: proportional step per detection, slew-rate and smoothing limits, deadband, lock timer, scan sweep when the target is lost, motion suppression while servos move (so it doesn't chase its own pan).
- Web UI on :80 (mode buttons, click-to-aim, sliders for every gain, invert/trim, laser/auto-fire, save to NVS) and MJPEG on :81. STA with AP fallback (`turret`/`turret123`), mDNS `turret.local`.
- Pins: D0 pan, D1 tilt, D2 laser, D3 button; GPIO21 LED status.
- Build results: PlatformIO 1.53 MB flash, face detection linked. Arduino core 3.3.7 via `make_arduino_sketch.ps1` + arduino-cli also compiles (1.04 MB), with face mode compiled out because core 3.x has no esp-dl.

**Enclosure** (`hardware/turret.scad`, OpenSCAD 2021.01)
- Five parts: `base` (pan servo drops through the top plate, cable hole, power inlet, vents), `base_lid`, `yoke` (horn pocket with radial screw slots, arm A with through-mounted tilt servo, arm B with M3 pivot), `head` (board clamped between front pads and lid posts, camera window, horn pocket, USB-C slot, 6 mm laser saddle), `head_lid` (friction lip + 2x M2).
- All parametric; the servo envelope, board stack and clearances are the first 90 lines. `render.ps1` / `render.sh` regenerate `stl/*.stl` plus `assembly.png` and `plate.png`.

## Things to check before printing

- `cam_dz` (lens offset on the Sense board) and `stack_h` are my estimates of the Sense board stack, not measured. Measure your board and set them; the head has 0.8 mm width slack and a 10 mm window.
- `sv_horn_h` (3.8) sets the head-to-arm gap. Measure your horn hub.
- Servo direction signs are unknown until mounted; use the Invert toggles in the UI.
- The head's USB-C slot wants a right-angle cable; a straight plug hits the disc on down-tilt. Soldered power leads through the lid grommet is the cleaner option.

No `secrets.h` exists yet; copy `include/secrets.h.example` and fill in WiFi, or leave it and use the AP.

# MG90D Micro Servo - Technical Specifications

## Dimensions & Physical Properties
- **Size**: 22.8 x 12.2 x 28.5 mm
- **Weight**: 13.4 grams
- **Spline Count**: 20

## Electrical & Performance
- **Operating Voltage**: 4.8V to 6V DC
- **Speed**: 0.1 sec/60° (at 4.8V) or 0.08 sec/60° (at 6.0V)
- **Stall Torque**: 
  - At 4.8V: 2.1 kg-cm (25 oz-in)
  - At 6.0V: 2.4 kg-cm (30.5 oz-in)

## Range & Control
- **Rotation**: Approximately 90 degrees total (45° in each direction)
- **Pulse Requirements**: Standard servo library defaults (1-2ms) don't provide full range; recommended custom pulse lengths are "0.75ms to 2.25ms"
- **Position Reference**: 1.5ms = center, ~2ms = full right, ~1ms = full left

## Key Features
The servo features "a new digital 13g metal gear, with a double ball bearing servo" (updated November 7, 2018). It includes multiple horns and mounting hardware.

**Note**: No CAD files or detailed drawing dimensions were provided in the available product documentation.

# Servo Motor OpenSCAD Model - Variable Definitions

Here are all the variable definitions from this micro servo 3D model:

```openscad
$a  = 32.70;  // height bottom to top of spline
$b  = 23.90;  // length short side to side
$cb = 22.50;  // height of body
$ct =  5.96;  // height of turret
$d  = 12.70;  // width long side to side
$e  = 32.60;  // length, including wings
$f  = 18.30;  // height bottom to underside of wings
$wt =  2.80;  // thickness of wings
$wd =  2.00;  // diameter of screw holes
$wc =  2.00;  // distance from wing border
$sd =  4.80;  // diameter
```

**Key dimensions:**
- Body: 23.90mm (short) × 12.70mm (wide) × 22.50mm (tall)
- Wings extend 32.60mm total length; positioned 18.30mm from bottom
- Wing thickness: 2.80mm; mounting holes: 2.00mm diameter
- Turret (boss): 12.70mm diameter × 5.96mm height
- Spline: 4.80mm diameter × 6.24mm height (calculated from $a - $cb - $ct)

The model represents a Kuman MG90S / Tower Pro SG90 micro servo motor.

## Claude  `16:42:30`

Updated the design for the MG90S. The Amazon listing has no drawing, so I pulled the TowerPro MG90S configuration table (A 32.5 ear span, B 22.8 body, C 28.4 bottom to spline top, D 12.4 width, F 18.5 bottom to ear top) and cross-checked against a community-measured MG90S clone (23.9 x 12.7 body, ears 2.3 mm higher). The two disagree, so the CAD is now tolerant of that spread rather than tuned to one of them.

**CAD changes** (`hardware/turret.scad`, STLs re-exported)
- Servo block rewritten to MG90S: boss 12.0 dia x 2.5 tall, spline 3.2 (sum matches C 28.4), flange bottom 16.0, hole spacing 27.8, 20T spline 4.8.
- Body through-cuts (base plate, arm A) use a loose 23.6 x 12.8 envelope + 0.3 so both the TowerPro and the fat clone fit; the ear screws locate the servo.
- Ear pilot holes are slots (27.3 to 28.3 mm spacing).
- Head-to-arm gap carries `gap_margin = 0.8`; if the hub height is wrong the horn seats slightly short instead of the head rubbing the arm.
- The boss passes the arm cut regardless of where the ears sit, so the flange-position disagreement does not affect the yoke.

**Firmware**: `SERVO_MIN_US/MAX_US` changed from 500/2500 to 544/2400. The TowerPro sheet says 1 to 2 ms for +-90; 500/2500 on an MG90S will drive into the end stops. Rebuilt, still 1.53 MB.

**README**: BOM, servo dimension notes and a pulse-range section updated.

**One measurement you still need to make**: `sv_horn_h`, the distance from the top of the round gear-head boss to the outer face of the cross horn when it is pushed fully onto the spline. Default is 4.8 mm. Put the real number in `turret.scad` line 55 before printing the yoke or head; it sets the arm A to head spacing directly.

Sources: [TowerPro MG90S](https://www.towerpro.com.tw/product/mg90s-3/), [TowerPro MG90S datasheet PDF](https://www.electronicoscaldas.com/datasheet/MG90S_Tower-Pro.pdf), [christophevg/3d-servo-model](https://github.com/christophevg/3d-servo-model/blob/master/model.scad), [Adafruit MG90D](https://www.adafruit.com/product/1143)

## Claude  `17:06:23`

Done. Two deliverables plus a material answer.

## Flash Studio project

`F:\turret2\hardware\turret_creator5pro.3mf`, open with File > Open Project (or drag onto the plate).

- Printer `Flashforge Creator 5 Pro 0.4 nozzle`, process `Turret 0.20mm Optimized @FF C5` (your existing preset: 0.2 mm, 3 walls, 15 % gyroid, 4 top/bottom, no brim, no support), filament `Flashforge PETG Basic @FF C5P`, bed type High Temp Plate (85 C).
- Five parts placed unrotated on the 256 x 256 bed with 13 mm+ margins; `turret_creator5pro_plate.png` shows the first-layer footprint.
- Test-sliced through the Flash Studio CLI: 3 h 13 min, 72.8 g, nothing outside the bed, no warnings.
- `hardware/make_project.py --slice` rebuilds it from `stl/` whenever the CAD changes.

Flash Studio is Orca-Flashforge 2.4.2; its CLI rejects the shipped profile JSONs until the `inherits` chain is flattened and a `type` field is added, so the script does that first.

## CAD fixes found while slicing

- The slicer flagged a floating cantilever on the base: the side power inlet was a closed 13 mm window. It is now a notch open to the bottom rim (no bridge; the breakout slides in from below and the lid closes it).
- The head was 18.5 mm deep but the 21 mm horn pocket needs the side face to be wider than that; the pocket was breaking through the front and back edges and would have collided with the lid. Head is now 24 mm deep (interior 20 mm, lid posts 3 x 3 mm).
- `turret.scad` had been renamed to `turret-.scad` in the folder at 16:40; I renamed it back since the render scripts and README reference `turret.scad`.

## PLA or PETG

PETG. The parts that take load are thin: 3 mm yoke arms, 2.3 mm of wall behind the horn pocket, M2 self-tapping pilots, a friction lip under constant preload. PETG's layer adhesion and toughness suit those better than PLA, and it does not creep in a warm enclosure. PLA prints with tighter tolerances and better bridges; if you use it anyway, expect the head lid lip to be snugger and keep the same process preset. The project ships with the Flashforge PETG Basic profile; swap to a PLA profile in Flash Studio if you change your mind, the layout and process carry over.

## Claude  `17:17:22`

The honest answer: only one of them is a considered choice, and none are specific to this design in a way that matters.

Stock `0.20mm Standard @FF C5` vs what I set:

| Setting | Stock | Override | Assessment |
|---|---|---|---|
| wall_loops | 3 | 3 | identical, redundant |
| enable_support | 0 | 0 | identical, redundant |
| elefant_foot_compensation | 0.1 | 0.1 | identical, redundant |
| brim_type | auto_brim | no_brim | the only deliberate call: all five parts have flat, wide first layers, so a brim only adds cleanup on the head's front face and the base rim. Auto-brim would probably add one to the yoke (tall arms on a 56 mm disc), which is harmless. Marginal either way. |
| sparse_infill | 15 % grid | 20 % gyroid | not justified here. Every thin section (3 mm arms, 2 mm shell walls, 2.3 mm behind the horn pocket) is 100 % perimeter at 3 walls; infill only exists inside the 3 mm base plate and 3.5 mm disc, where it is irrelevant to strength. |
| top / bottom shells | 5 / 3 | 4 / 4 | not justified. Bottom 3 vs 4 layers changes nothing on these parts; top 5 to 4 is slightly weaker for no reason. |

I carried those numbers over from the spirit of the deleted preset rather than deriving them from the geometry. The parts' strength comes from wall count, which the stock preset already sets to 3.

Recommendation: drop all overrides and ship the project on the unmodified stock process (auto brim included). Want me to do that, or keep `no_brim` only?

## Claude  `17:27:59`

Written to `F:\turret2\BOM.md` and linked from the README.

## BOM (board and servos excluded)

Required:

| Qty | Item | Link |
|---|---|---|
| 1 kg | FLASHFORGE PETG Pro 1.75 mm black (use the `PETG Pro @FF C5P` preset for this spool) | [B07Y9S22P5](https://www.amazon.com/FLASHFORGE-Filament-Dimensional-Mechanical-Waterproof/dp/B07Y9S22P5) |
| 10x M2x8, 4x M2x5 | M2 self-tapping screw assortment, 800 pcs | [B0CY986CCR](https://www.amazon.com/Tapping-Woodworking-Fastener-Drilling-Assortment/dp/B0CY986CCR) |
| 1 | M3x12 button-head socket screw (tilt pivot) | [B07QDWDB5D](https://www.amazon.com/M3x12-Button-Socket-Screws-Stainless/dp/B07QDWDB5D) |
| 1 | 470 uF 16 V electrolytic, pack of 5 | [B07YN5MZBY](https://www.amazon.com/Projects-Radial-Electrolytic-Capacitor-470uF/dp/B07YN5MZBY) |
| 1 | 5 V 3 A USB-C power adapter, UL listed | [B09JW4QQJ2](https://www.amazon.com/Security-01-Supply-Adapter-Type-C-MLF-C060503000CU/dp/B09JW4QQJ2) |
| 1 | USB-C panel-mount female breakout (V/GND pads), for the base rim notch | [B0H9S9C73Q](https://www.amazon.com/Amabro-Connector-Female-Socket-Breakout/dp/B0H9S9C73Q) |

Optional:

| Qty | Item | Link |
|---|---|---|
| 1 kit | 22 AWG silicone hookup wire, 6 colors | [B07WYYDBZP](https://www.amazon.com/BINNEKER-Silicone-Resistant-Electronic-Stranded/dp/B07WYYDBZP) |
| 4 | 10 x 3 mm clear rubber feet | [B07JFGW1XC](https://www.amazon.com/Bumpers-Alamic-Adhesive-Transparent-Dampening/dp/B07JFGW1XC) |
| 1 | 650 nm 5 V laser module, 6 mm barrel, pack of 10 | [B071FT9HSV](https://www.amazon.com/HiLetgo-10pcs-650nm-Diode-Laser/dp/B071FT9HSV) |
| 1 | 12 mm pre-wired momentary push button (trigger) | [B0C8HM4T24](https://www.amazon.com/DaierTek-Momentary-Button-Switch-Waterproof/dp/B0C8HM4T24) |
| 1 | Right-angle USB-C to A cable, 1 ft (flashing in the head) | [B0784F9JP2](https://www.amazon.com/SUNGUY-Braided-Charging-Samsung-OnePlus/dp/B0784F9JP2) |

Two things the BOM notes flag: the laser draws ~20 mA, so drive it through a small transistor rather than straight from GPIO3; and the trigger button needs a 12 mm hole that is not in the CAD.

## How the turret operates

**Power-up.** Both servos are driven to 90/90 (mechanical center) immediately. The camera initializes at 240x240 RGB565. WiFi tries the SSID in `secrets.h` for 15 s (LED blinks fast), then falls back to its own AP `turret`/`turret123` (LED blinks slowly). The web UI is at `http://turret.local/` or `192.168.4.1` in AP mode. Saved settings (gains, inversions, trims) load from flash.

**Frame pipeline.** One task grabs frames continuously. Per frame it runs whichever detector the mode asks for, hands the result to the tracker, draws the overlay (crosshair, deadband box, target box, SCAN/LOCK text), JPEG-encodes, and publishes. The MJPEG stream on port 81 and `/capture` just serve the latest published frame, so the video shows exactly what the tracker saw.

**Modes** (buttons or keys 1-4):
- *Manual*: no detection. Aim by clicking the video, arrow keys, the D-pad, or the pan/tilt sliders. Click-to-aim moves proportionally toward the clicked point.
- *Face*: esp-dl two-stage face detector; the largest face is the target.
- *Motion*: the frame is split into 30x30 cells of 8x8 px; cells whose brightness changed more than the threshold vote for a centroid. Because the camera moves with the turret, motion detection is muted while the servos move and for `settle` ms afterward, otherwise it would chase its own panning.
- *Scan*: no detection, pan sweeps end to end at `scan speed` with tilt at `scan tilt`.

**Tracking law.** The target's offset from frame center is normalized to -1..1 per axis. Outside the `deadband` (pixels), the servo setpoint moves by `kp x error` degrees; inside it, nothing. The 50 Hz control task then slews the actual servo position toward the setpoint, limited to `max step` degrees per tick and eased by `smoothing`, so a big detection jump becomes a controlled sweep instead of a slam. Because the camera is on the moving head, this is an incremental controller: each frame corrects the residual error, and it converges when the target sits inside the deadband. If the turret runs away from the target, the sign for that axis is wrong; toggle Invert pan/tilt.

**Lost and lock.** If nothing is detected for `lost` ms and "scan when lost" is on, the pan starts sweeping until something is found. If the target stays inside the deadband for `lock ms`, the state becomes LOCK: red box, LED solid, and with Auto-fire on, the laser output (D2) turns on. The laser can also be toggled manually (button, key L, or the physical trigger on D3).

**Persistence.** Every slider takes effect live. "Save settings" writes them to flash; "Defaults" restores the built-ins without touching camera flip/mirror.

## User  `17:33:55`

when lock is achieved, it fires the laser at the position detected as the face?

## Claude  `17:34:08`

Yes, with one qualification: the laser is fixed to the head, so it fires wherever the camera's optical center is pointing, not at a computed coordinate. LOCK means the face center has been inside the deadband around the frame center for `lock ms`, which means the head is already aimed at the face. With Auto-fire on, the laser turns on at that moment and stays on while LOCK holds; it goes off as soon as the face drifts out of the deadband or is lost.

What limits the accuracy of the dot landing on the face:

1. **Parallax.** The laser saddle sits about 20 mm above the camera lens on the head. At 2 m the dot lands 20 mm above the camera's aim point; at 0.5 m the same 20 mm. That offset is constant in space, not in angle, so it matters at short range and is negligible at distance. Aim the laser module slightly downward in its saddle (the 6.3 mm bore has room) to zero it at your typical range, or tilt trim.

2. **Deadband.** The default is 12 px in a 240 px frame. With the OV2640's roughly 60 degree field of view that is about 3 degrees, so LOCK can be declared with the face up to 3 degrees off axis. Reduce `deadband` for tighter aim at the cost of more hunting; the face box itself is 40 to 80 px wide at conversational distances, so 12 px still lands inside the face.

3. **Target point.** The tracker aims at the center of the face bounding box, roughly the bridge of the nose. That is the point the laser would land on when parallax is zeroed.

That last point is the reason for a caution: a 5 mW laser on a person's face is an eye hazard. If this is going to point at people, use an LED in the saddle or aim the laser module a fixed amount below the camera axis so the dot lands on the chest. The firmware treats the output as a generic "fire" pin, so nothing changes electrically.

## User  `17:36:42`

once lock is achieved, and target slowly moves, the turret will follow the target maintaining the laser dot on the chest (assuming that parallax is set)?

## Claude  `17:37:20`

Not quite, with the code as written. Two gaps:

1. **Lock drops during every correction.** Currently LOCK requires the face to be inside the deadband, and correction only starts when it leaves the deadband. So a slowly walking target produces: face drifts to the deadband edge, LOCK drops (laser off), servo nudges it back to center, `lock ms` elapses, LOCK returns (laser on). The dot would blink off for roughly half a second at every step.

2. **"Chest" is not a fixed angle.** A fixed downward tilt of the laser puts the dot 35 cm below the face only at one distance; at half that distance it is on the neck, at twice it is on the belly. Since the face width in pixels scales with 1/distance the same way, the right way is to aim the camera at a point `N` face-widths below the face center in software (chest is about 2.3 face-widths below the eyes), with the laser boresighted parallel to the camera. That offset is distance-independent.

I'll fix both: lock hysteresis (lock releases only outside a wider radius, correction continues while locked) and an aim-offset in face-widths.

## Claude  `17:38:48`

Now it does. Firmware rebuilt (1.53 MB, success) with two changes:

**Lock hysteresis.** Correction now keeps running while locked. The lock is declared when the aim point sits inside the deadband for `lock ms`, and released only when the error exceeds `deadband x lock release` (default 3x, so 36 px) or the target is lost. A slowly walking target is followed in small nudges with the laser held on; only a fast excursion drops the lock.

**Aim offset in face-widths.** New setting `aim below` (default 0). In Face mode the tracker centers the frame on a point that many face-widths below the face center. Because face width in pixels and any body offset in pixels both scale with 1/distance, the ratio is constant: roughly 2.3 puts the aim point on the chest whether the person is at 1 m or 4 m. With that, do not tilt the laser in its saddle; boresight it parallel to the camera and let the software carry the offset. The overlay draws the aim point as a small cross tethered to the face so you can see where the dot will land before enabling Auto-fire.

So with `aim below` at ~2.3, Auto-fire on, and the laser aligned to the camera axis, the sequence is: face found, head slews until the chest point is at frame center, `lock ms` later the laser comes on, and it stays on and follows as the person moves, until they move faster than the tracker can follow or leave the frame.

Limits to be aware of:

- The face must stay in the frame for detection. At 2.3 face-widths the face sits near the top of the 240 px frame; that works out to roughly 0.75 m minimum range with the OV2640's field of view. Closer than that, reduce `aim below`.
- Tracking rate is the face-detector rate, about 5 to 8 fps. Someone walking at normal pace across the field at 3 m moves ~15 deg/s, which is inside the servo slew limit but the tracker corrects only every 150 ms, so expect the dot to lag a few centimetres behind a mover and catch up when they slow.
- The 20 mm laser-to-lens parallax is not compensated; at 1 m it is a 20 mm high dot, irrelevant on a chest.

Both settings are in the web UI under Tracking and persist with Save.

## User  `17:40:11`

are you expecting the target to walk across the coverage area, sideways, while facing the camera??

## Claude  `17:41:24`

No. That was a wrong assumption and it undermines the whole "follow" claim. The face detector only fires on a roughly frontal face (some yaw, not profile), and with the current code the lock drops the instant a frame has no face. A person walking across the field, turning, or looking away would be lost within a frame or two, and the turret would sit there or start scanning.

The right structure is: use the face only to *acquire*, then track the *torso* by color, which does not care which way the person faces and does not depend on the background (so it keeps working while the head moves, unlike frame-difference motion). Face detection then runs only periodically to re-anchor the track. I'll implement that now: a CAMShift-style hue/saturation tracker seeded from the region below the detected face, with the aim point carried along at a fixed proportion of the torso window.

## Claude  `17:49:27`

Implemented and built. "Face" mode is now "Person" mode with a two-stage tracker.

**How it works now**

1. *Acquire*: the esp-dl face detector runs every frame until it finds a face.
2. *Seed*: a color model is built from the torso region below the face (1.6 face-widths wide, 1.4 face-heights tall, starting below the chin). It is a hue/saturation histogram taken as a *ratio* against the whole frame, so shirt colors that also appear in the background are down-weighted.
3. *Follow*: every frame, a CAMShift-style mean-shift window is pulled onto the densest matching area. This needs neither a frontal face nor a static background, so it keeps working while the person turns, looks away, walks sideways, and while the servos are moving the camera. It runs in a few milliseconds, so between face checks the pipeline runs at camera rate (10-20 fps) instead of 5-8.
4. *Re-anchor*: the face detector re-runs every `face re-detect ms` (default 1 s). A found face re-centers the window and blends 30 % of a fresh color model in (lighting drift). A missed face changes nothing; the torso track carries on.
5. *Aim*: the chest aim point (`aim below`, now default 2.3 face-widths) is recorded at seed time as a fraction of the torso window and rides along with it, so LOCK and Auto-fire behave the same whether the current frame came from the face or the torso path.
6. *Lost*: if the color match falls below `torso min confidence` for `lost` ms, the track is dropped, the scan sweep starts, and the next face seeds a new track.

**Verification**

- PlatformIO build: 1.54 MB, success. Arduino core 3.3.7 build also compiles.
- Native unit test of the color tracker (MSVC, synthetic 240x240 RGB565 frames): a blue-shirted target moved across the frame in 12 steps past a same-hue distractor poster; tracked within 1 px at every step, and correctly reported lost when the target was removed rather than jumping to the poster.

**Honest limits**

- It is a color tracker. Two people in identical shirts, or a shirt matching a large background area, will confuse it between re-detects; the 1 s face re-anchor bounds how far it can wander but cannot fix an ambiguous scene.
- Acquisition still needs one frontal-ish face view. Someone entering with their back to the camera is not acquired until they turn.
- Untested on real hardware. The thresholds (`torso min confidence` 0.12, re-detect 1 s) are starting points; both are live sliders in the UI.

The README describes the mode, the new settings, and these limits.

**Tool: Bash** — Append hardware bring-up findings to the README

```json
{
 "command": "cat >> /f/turret2/README.md <<'EOF'\n\n## Bring-up notes (2026-09-10, board on the XIAO Expansion Board, no servos)\n\n- Build/flash: `pio run -e xiao_expansion -t upload --upload-port COM6`. The expansion env moves the\n  tilt servo to D2 and the laser to D6 because the expansion board owns D1 (user button) and D3\n  (buzzer); it adds the SSD1306 status page (OLED found at 0x3C) and buzzer cues.\n- Camera image was upside down in this mounting: set H-mirror and V-flip in the UI and Save. The\n  head-mounted orientation will differ; set it once there.\n- Crash found and fixed: panic \"Stack canary watchpoint triggered (cam_task)\". The camera driver's\n  2 KB task logs `EV-VSYNC-OVF` through vprintf whenever a frame is held too long; the newlib lock\n  setup inside that printf overflows the stack. Fixes: the detector now copies each frame to PSRAM\n  and returns the driver buffer immediately, the driver gets 3 buffers, and the IDF log vprintf is\n  replaced with a no-op (`esp_log_level_set` was not sufficient in this SDK build).\n- LEDC: ESP32-S3 allows at most 14-bit PWM resolution; the servo driver asked for 16 and silently\n  failed. Now 14-bit (1.2 us per step at 50 Hz).\n- The esp-dl face model does not detect masked faces. Acquisition needs an unmasked, roughly\n  frontal face; a mask blocks Person mode acquisition entirely (torso tracking still works once\n  acquired).\n- Pipeline rates on the S3: 10 fps in Manual (deliberately capped), 15-16 fps in Person mode while\n  no face is present (stage-1 detector 6-14 ms), stream to a PC over the AP at the sa
... [201 more chars]

## Claude  `18:47:07`

Flashed and running on COM6. Summary of the session on hardware:

**Libraries, since you asked:** everything talking to the Sense board is what Seeed's own examples use: the `esp32-camera` driver bundled in arduino-esp32 with the `CAMERA_MODEL_XIAO_ESP32S3` pin map, esp-dl face models from the same core, U8g2 for the expansion board's SSD1306 (Seeed's expansion-board wiki uses U8g2/U8x8), and LEDC directly for the servos. Nothing third-party beyond U8g2.

**What the board reports now** (`xiao_expansion` env): PSRAM 8 MB, face detection linked, OLED found at 0x3C, AP `turret` at 192.168.4.1, 10 fps Manual / 15 fps Person, heap steady at ~159 KB, no resets over a 60 s Person-mode run. This PC is joined to the turret AP (profile `turret`, PC at 192.168.4.2); `netsh wlan disconnect` drops it.

**Bugs found on hardware and fixed:**
1. Panic `Stack canary watchpoint triggered (cam_task)`, reproduced twice and decoded from the backtrace: the camera driver's 2 KB task was logging its VSYNC-overflow warning through vprintf/stdio and blowing its stack. Fix: the detector copies each frame to PSRAM and returns the driver's buffer at once, the driver gets 3 buffers, and the IDF log vprintf is a no-op. A bare `cam_hal: EV-VSYNC-OVF` line still prints occasionally via the ROM printf; harmless.
2. Servo PWM setup failed silently: ESP32-S3 LEDC max is 14 bits, I had asked for 16. Now 14-bit (1.2 us steps). Matters tomorrow when the servos arrive.
3. Image was upside-down in this mounting; H-mirror + V-flip are set and saved to NVS.
4. NVS first-boot noise silenced (defaults written once).

**Why no detection during my runs:** the frames I pulled show you in an N95 mask, and the esp-dl face model does not fire on masked faces. Nothing else blocks acquisition.

**To test it yourself right now:** open `http://192.168.4.1/` in a browser on this PC, click **Person**, take the mask off and look at the camera from 0.5 to 2 m. Expect: green face box, then a cyan torso box that persists while you turn or move sideways, the aim cross 2.3 face-widths below the face, `LOCK` after 0.6 s, a beep from the expansion board on acquire and on lock, and the OLED showing mode, target and fps. Without servos the pan value will sweep (scan) until it sees you, then hold.

Everything is written up in the README's "Bring-up notes" section.
